\subsection{Cayley modes and coefficient algebra}
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Uniform one-dimensional Cayley Taylor remainder]%
\begin{lemma}[Uniform one-dimensional Cayley Taylor remainder]%
\label{lem:uniform-one-dimensional-cayley-taylor-remainder}
Let \(L\ge1\).  The Taylor coefficients of \(R_\varepsilon(y)\) at
\(\varepsilon=0\) satisfy
\[
  R_\varepsilon(y)=\sum_{n=0}^{L}u_n(y)\varepsilon^n
  +\varepsilon^{L+1}v_L(y,\varepsilon)
\]
for all \(y,\varepsilon\in\RR\), where \(v_L\) is a rational function on
\(\RR^2\) and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-vL-one-dimensional-bound}
  \sup_{y\in\RR,\ |\varepsilon|\le1}|v_L(y,\varepsilon)|
  \le \sup_{y,\varepsilon\in\RR}|v_L(y,\varepsilon)|
  \le \frac{5L+7}{2}.
\end{equation}
Moreover
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-un-one-dimensional-bound}
  \|u_n\|_{L^\infty(\RR)}\le n+1,\qquad 0\le n\le L+1 .
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Uniform derivative bound for the Cayley quotient]%
\begin{lemma}[Uniform derivative bound for the Cayley quotient]%
\label{lem:uniform-cayley-derivative-bound}
For every \(L\ge2\),
\[
\sup_{y\in\RR,\ |\varepsilon|\le1}
\left|
\partial_\varepsilon^{L+1}
\frac{1+y^2}{1+(y-\varepsilon)^2}
\right|<\infty .
\]
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Cayley--Chebyshev mode formula]%
\begin{theorem}[Cayley--Chebyshev mode formula]%
\label{thm:cayley-chebyshev-mode}
\label{lem:mode-parity-vanish}
Let \(U_n\) be the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind.  If
\(y=\tan\theta\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cayley-chebyshev-mode}
u_n(\tan\theta)=\cos^n\theta\,U_n(\sin\theta).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, for \(n\ge1\),
\[
u_n(\tan\theta)=
\cos^{n-1}\theta\,
\sin\!\left((n+1)\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta\right)\right),
\]
and therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cayley-mode-parity}
u_n(-y)=(-1)^nu_n(y).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{definition}[Canonical coefficient finiteness]
\begin{definition}[Canonical coefficient finiteness]
\label{def:canonical-coefficient-finiteness}
Let \(k\ge4\), and let \(\nu\) be a probability law on \(\RR\) with finite
mean.  We say that \(A_k(\nu)\) is \emph{finite}, and write
\(A_k(\nu)\in\RR\), when the reduced polynomial
\(A_k^{\rm form}\) admits its canonical Lebesgue evaluation at the centred
moments of \(\nu\): every moment occurring in a nonzero monomial is an
absolutely integrable, finite real number, and ordinary real arithmetic then
gives \eqref{eq:universal-entropy-coefficient-evaluation}.  Otherwise we
write \(A_k(\nu)\notin\RR\).  In particular, no value is assigned by a
principal value, by a choice of truncation, or by cancellation of divergent
quantities.  This is a deliberately specified domain for evaluation of the
universal coefficient polynomial.  It is not defined through the existence
of an entropy remainder.  Theorem~\ref{thm:poisson-kl-even-orders} separately
identifies the evaluated polynomial with an entropy-asymptotic coefficient
under its finite-moment hypothesis.
\end{definition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Universal entropy coefficients]%
\begin{theorem}[Universal entropy coefficients]%
\label{thm:universal-entropy-coefficients}
The coefficient of \(t^{-k}\) in the formal expression
\[
\int_{\RR}\left\{
\left(1+\sum_{n=2}^{k}\mathfrak m_nu_n(y)t^{-n}\right)
\log\left(1+\sum_{n=2}^{k}\mathfrak m_nu_n(y)t^{-n}\right)
-\sum_{n=2}^{k}\mathfrak m_nu_n(y)t^{-n}
\right\}\omega(\dd y)
\]
is \(A_k^{\rm form}\).  If the moments needed in
\eqref{eq:universal-entropy-coefficient} exist for \(\nu\), then
\[
A_k(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{n_1+\cdots+n_r=k\\ n_j\ge2}}
\mu_{n_1}\cdots\mu_{n_r}\,J(n_1,\ldots,n_r).
\]
All odd formal coefficients vanish.
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Finite Fourier algorithm for mode integrals]%
\begin{theorem}[Finite Fourier algorithm for mode integrals]%
\label{thm:finite-fourier-mode-integral-algorithm}
Define \(Q_n(e^{2\ii\theta})=u_n(\tan\theta)\).  Equivalently, for every
\(n\ge1\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mode-laurent-polynomial-definition}
Q_n(z)=2^{-n}(-\ii)^n
\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n-1}{k-1}
\bigl(z^k+(-1)^nz^{-k}\bigr).
\end{equation}
Thus, if \(Q_n(z)=\sum_kq_{n,k}z^k\), then \(q_{n,0}=0\) and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mode-laurent-coefficients}
q_{n,k}=
2^{-n}(-\ii)^n\operatorname{sgn}(k)^{\,n\bmod2}
\binom{n-1}{|k|-1},
\qquad 0<|k|\le n,
\end{equation}
with \(q_{n,k}=0\) for \(|k|>n\).
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mode-integral-constant-term}
J(n_1,\ldots,n_r)=[z^0]\prod_{j=1}^r Q_{n_j}(z).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Minimal absolute moment for universal polynomial evaluation]%
\begin{proposition}[Minimal absolute moment for universal polynomial evaluation]%
\label{thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy}
Let \(m\ge2\), and let \(\nu\) be a probability law on \(\RR\) with finite
mean \(\bar\gamma\).  Put \(X_c=\gamma-\bar\gamma\).  No variance or tail
assumption is imposed.  For \(m\ge3\), the reduced universal polynomial has
the form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-moment-equivalence-top-layer}
A_{2m}^{\rm form}
=\kappa_m\,\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}
 +P_m(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{2m-3}),
\qquad
\kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0,
\end{equation}
At \(m=2\), \(A_4^{\rm form}=\mathfrak m_2^2/8\).  Thus the exact top absolute moment
is sufficient for evaluating every monomial and is minimal among
absolute-moment assumptions that guarantee evaluation uniformly over the full
class of centred laws.  Equivalently, within the specially stipulated
canonical domain of Definition~\ref{def:canonical-coefficient-finiteness},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy}
 A_{2m}(\nu)\in\RR
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \EE|X_c|^{2m-2}<\infty .
\end{equation}
This equivalence describes only the domain of polynomial evaluation.  For
\(m\ge3\) it is not an unconditional converse for an entropy remainder.  When the
finite-moment condition holds, Theorem~\ref{thm:poisson-kl-even-orders}, with
\(N=m\), identifies this finite number as the actual \(t^{-2m}\)
coefficient in the entropy expansion under precisely the same finite
\((2m-2)\)nd absolute-moment hypothesis.
\end{proposition}

\subsection{One-dimensional coefficient theorem}
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Exact-moment density truncation]%
\begin{lemma}[Exact-moment density truncation]%
\label{lem:exact-moment-density-truncation}
Let \(L\ge2\), and let \(\nu\) have mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred
\(L\)th absolute moment.  For
\[
\delta_t(y):=
\frac{(P_t*\nu)(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}{P_t(ty)}-1
\]
one has
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-moment-density-truncation}
\delta_t(y)=\sum_{n=2}^{L}\mu_nu_n(y)t^{-n}+\rho_{L,t}(y),
\qquad
\|\rho_{L,t}\|_{\infty}+\|\rho_{L,t}\|_{L^1(\omega)}=o(t^{-L}).
\end{equation}
Also \(\|\delta_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2})\).
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Finite quotient jet implies KL coefficient jet]%
\begin{proposition}[Finite quotient jet implies KL coefficient jet]%
\label{prop:finite-quotient-jet-implies-kl-coefficient-jet}
Let \((E,\mathcal E,\lambda)\) be a probability space, let \(J\ge2\), and
let \(b_2,\ldots,b_J\in L^\infty(\lambda)\).  Put
\[
  q_t=\sum_{j=2}^{J}b_jt^{-j},\qquad
  \delta_t=q_t+\rho_t,
\]
and assume
\[
  \|\delta_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2}),\qquad
  \|\rho_t\|_\infty+\|\rho_t\|_{L^1(\lambda)}=o(t^{-J}).
\]
Then, with \(\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-quotient-jet-transfer}
  \int_E\Phi(\delta_t)\,\dd\lambda
  =
  \sum_{m=4}^{J+2}C_m t^{-m}+o(t^{-(J+2)}),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-quotient-jet-coefficients}
  C_m=
  \sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}
  \frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
  \sum_{\substack{j_1+\cdots+j_r=m\\2\le j_1,\ldots,j_r\le J}}
  \int_E b_{j_1}\cdots b_{j_r}\,\dd\lambda .
\end{equation}
If a symmetry of \(\lambda\) and the functions \(b_j\) makes the integrals in
\eqref{eq:finite-quotient-jet-coefficients} vanish for odd \(m\), then the
corresponding odd coefficients in \eqref{eq:finite-quotient-jet-transfer}
vanish.

More generally, let \(s_t\downarrow0\) be a comparison scale.  Suppose
\[
  \delta_t=a_t+B_t+e_t,\qquad
  a_t=\sum_{j=2}^{J}b_jt^{-j},
\]
\[
  \|a_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2}),\qquad
  \|B_t\|_\infty+\|e_t\|_\infty=o(t^{-2}),\qquad
  \|e_t\|_{L^1(\lambda)}=o(s_t/t^{-2}).
\]
If, for some fixed Taylor order \(R_0\), the finite polynomial
\[
  \sum_{r=2}^{R_0}\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}(a_t+B_t)^r
\]
has only finitely many monomial contributions at scale \(s_t\), then
\(\int_E\Phi(\delta_t)\,\dd\lambda\) has exactly those finite monomial
contributions at scale \(s_t\); every monomial containing \(e_t\) is
\(o(s_t)\), and the Taylor remainder is \(o(s_t)\) provided
\(\|\delta_t\|_\infty^{R_0}\|\delta_t\|_{L^1(\lambda)}=o(s_t)\).
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Moving-scale Taylor transfer]%
\begin{lemma}[Moving-scale Taylor transfer]%
\label{lem:moving-scale-taylor-transfer}
Let \((E,\mathcal E,\lambda)\) be a probability space, let \(p\ge4\), and let
\(a_2,\ldots,a_{p-1},b\in L^\infty(\lambda)\).  Put
\[
  A_t=\sum_{j=2}^{p-1}a_jt^{-j},\qquad
  B_t=t^{-p}M_p(t)b,
\]
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moving-scale-growth}
  M_p(t)\to\infty,\qquad M_p(t)=o(t^{p-2}).
\end{equation}
Let \(e_t\in L^\infty(\lambda)\cap L^1(\lambda)\) satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moving-scale-error}
  \|e_t\|_\infty+\|e_t\|_{L^1(\lambda)}
  =o(t^{-p}M_p(t)).
\end{equation}
Then, with \(\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:moving-scale-transfer}
  \int_E\Phi(A_t+B_t+e_t)\,\dd\lambda
  =
  \int_E\Phi(A_t)\,\dd\lambda
  +t^{-(p+2)}M_p(t)\int_Ea_2b\,\dd\lambda
  +o(t^{-(p+2)}M_p(t)).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Finite density jet entropy transfer]%
\begin{proposition}[Finite density jet entropy transfer]%
\label{prop:finite-density-jet-entropy-transfer}
Let \(N\ge2\), put \(L=2N-2\), and let \((E,\mathcal E,\lambda)\) be a
probability space.  Suppose
\[
\delta_t=d_t+\rho_t,\qquad
d_t(y)=\sum_{n=2}^{L}a_n(y)t^{-n},
\]
where \(a_2,\ldots,a_L\in L^\infty(\lambda)\),
\(\|d_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2})\), and
\[
\|\rho_t\|_\infty+\|\rho_t\|_{L^1(\lambda)}=o(t^{-L}).
\]
Then, with \(\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s\),
\[
\int_E\Phi(\delta_t)\,\dd\lambda
=
\sum_{r=2}^{N}\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\int_E d_t(y)^r\,\dd\lambda
+o(t^{-(L+2)}).
\]
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Finite-moment Poisson entropy transfer and coefficient formula]%
\begin{theorem}[Finite-moment Poisson entropy transfer and coefficient formula]%
\label{thm:poisson-kl-even-orders}
Let \(N\ge2\), put \(L=2N-2\), and let \(\nu\) be a probability law on
\(\RR\) with finite mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred \(L\)th absolute
moment.  Let
\[
\begin{aligned}
X_c&=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},\\
\mu_n&=\int_{\RR} X_c^n\,\dd\nu(\gamma)\qquad(2\le n\le L),\\
h_t&=P_t*\nu,\qquad
g_t(x)&=P_t(x-\bar{\gamma}),
\end{aligned}
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:poisson-kl-even-orders}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=\sum_{m=2}^{N}A_{2m}(\nu)t^{-2m}+o(t^{-2N}).
\end{equation}
For \(4\le k\le2N\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:coefficient-identification-transfer-expansion}
A_k(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{n_1+\cdots+n_r=k\\2\le n_j\le2N-2}}
\mu_{n_1}\cdots\mu_{n_r}
\int_{\RR}u_{n_1}\cdots u_{n_r}\,\dd\omega .
\end{equation}
All retained odd \(A_k\) vanish.
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Positive-margin quotient remainder and entropy transfer]
\begin{lemma}[Positive-margin quotient remainder and entropy transfer]
\label{lem:positive-margin-quotient-remainder}
\label{rem:positive-margin-retained-order}
\label{prop:density-to-entropy-descent}
\label{lem:closed-cayley-mode-constant-envelopes}
Let \(L\ge2\), \(0<\eta\le1\), and let \(\nu\) have mean
\(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred \((L+\eta)\)th absolute moment.  With
\[
\delta_t(y):=
\frac{(P_t*\nu)(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}{P_t(ty)}-1
\]
there is a remainder \(\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}\) such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-margin-density-remainder}
\delta_t(y)=
\sum_{n=2}^{L}\mu_nu_n(y)t^{-n}+\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}(y),
\qquad
\|\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}\|_{\infty}
+\|\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}\|_{L^1(\omega)}
=O_\nu(t^{-(L+\eta)}).
\end{equation}
Moreover \(\|\delta_t\|_\infty=O_\nu(t^{-2})\).
More precisely, the remainder satisfies
\[
\|\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}\|_{\infty}
+\|\rho_{L,t}^{(\eta)}\|_{L^1(\omega)}
\le
C_{L,\eta}\,\EE|X_c|^{L+\eta}\,t^{-(L+\eta)},
\]
where \(C_{L,\eta}\) depends only on \(L,\eta\) and the fixed Cayley-mode
envelopes.  For \(0<\eta<1\), this is a genuine fractional-moment
improvement; for \(\eta=1\), it is the usual \((L+1)\)-moment estimate.

Consequently, if \(N\ge2\), \(L=2N-2\), and
\(\EE|X_c|^{2N-2+\eta}<\infty\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:positive-margin-retained-order-theorem}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\|P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}))
=\sum_{m=2}^{N}A_{2m}(\nu)t^{-2m}
+O_\nu(t^{-(2N+\eta)}).
\end{equation}
\label{eq:explicit-entropy-constant-bound}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Lower moments do not identify the next even moment]%
\begin{lemma}[Lower moments do not identify the next even moment]%
\label{lem:lower-moment-realizability-obstruction}
Let \(L\ge4\) be even.  There exist two centred symmetric compactly
supported probability laws \(\nu_+\) and \(\nu_-\) on \(\RR\) such that
\[
  \int x^j\,\nu_+(\dd x)=\int x^j\,\nu_-(\dd x)
  \qquad(0\le j\le L-1),
\]
but
\[
  \int x^L\,\nu_+(\dd x)\ne \int x^L\,\nu_-(\dd x).
\]
In particular, even within compactly supported centred symmetric laws, the
moment vector through order \(L-1\) does not determine the \(L\)th centred
moment.
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Top-moment dependence of the universal coefficient]%
\begin{proposition}[Top-moment dependence of the universal coefficient]%
\label{prop:poisson-kl-moment-threshold-sharpness}
\label{prop:analytic-first-evaluability-obstruction}
For \(N\ge3\), the universal top coefficient \(A_{2N}^{\rm form}\) contains
the nonzero monomial
\[
\kappa_N\,\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2N-2},
\qquad
\kappa_N=(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}\ne0 .
\]
More precisely, with \(L=2N-2\),
\[
  A_{2N}^{\rm form}
  =\kappa_N\,\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_L
  +P_N(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{L-1}),
\]
where \(P_N\) involves no \(\mathfrak m_L\).  Consequently the displayed
universal polynomial \(A_{2N}\) cannot be evaluated from centred moments of
order at most \(2N-3\) on the full class of laws; two compactly supported
centred symmetric laws can have the same such lower moments but different
values of \(A_{2N}\).

This is the algebraic sharpness mechanism in
Theorem~\ref{thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy}.  It also proves the
following stronger non-identifiability statement: if the object to be
recovered is the same polynomial \(A_{2N}\) on the full finite-moment class,
then centred moments through order \(2N-3\) do not contain enough
information.  This does not assert a universal renormalized entropy residual
for arbitrary laws outside the coefficient domain.  At the regularly varying
moment boundary, however, the tail class selects a canonical integrated
slowly varying normalization; this case is closed by
Theorem~\ref{cor:main-body-conditional-missing-top-moment-tail}.  The
unconditional analytic tail threshold is the leading variance theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold}.
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Regularly varying missing-moment boundary layer]%
\begin{theorem}[Regularly varying missing-moment boundary layer]%
\label{cor:main-body-conditional-missing-top-moment-tail}
Let \(N\ge3\), put \(p=2N-2\), and let \(X\) have a centred probability
law \(\nu\) on \(\RR\).  Assume that, for some measurable eventually
positive slowly varying function \(L\) and constants \(c_+,c_-\ge0\) with
\(C:=c_++c_->0\),
\[
 \frac{\mathbb P(X>x)}{x^{-p}L(x)}\longrightarrow c_+,\qquad
 \frac{\mathbb P(X<-x)}{x^{-p}L(x)}\longrightarrow c_-,
 \qquad x\to\infty.
\]
Fix \(x_0>0\) beyond which \(L\) is positive, and assume that
\[
 \ell_L(t):=\int_{x_0}^{t}\frac{L(s)}s\,\dd s\longrightarrow\infty.
\]
Then \(\EE|X|^q<\infty\) for every \(q<p\), whereas
\(\EE|X|^p=\infty\), and the truncated boundary moment satisfies
\[
 M_p(t):=\EE\!\left[|X|^p\mathbf1_{\{|X|\le t\}}\right]
 \sim pC\ell_L(t).
\]
With \(H(t)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\|P_t)\) and
\(\kappa_N=(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}\), one has the exact boundary expansion
\[
H(t)=
\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}
+\kappa_N\mu_2\,t^{-2N}M_p(t)
+o(t^{-2N}M_p(t)).
\]
Equivalently,
\[
 \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{t^{2N}}{M_p(t)}
 \left\{H(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
 =\kappa_N\mu_2,
\]
and
\[
 \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{t^{2N}}{\ell_L(t)}
 \left\{H(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
 =p(c_++c_-)\kappa_N\mu_2.
\]
Thus the leading boundary layer depends on the sum of the two tail constants
but not on their balance.  In particular, \(L\equiv1\) gives a \(\log t\)
correction, while \(L(t)=(\log t)^{-1}\) gives a \(\log\log t\)
correction.  No density, symmetry, or monotonicity of \(L\) is assumed.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Sharp leading threshold}
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Second-mode transform of a Cauchy translate]%
\begin{lemma}[Second-mode transform of a Cauchy translate]%
\label{lem:second-mode-cauchy-translate-transform}
For \(\varepsilon\in\RR\),
\[
\int_{\RR}u_2(y)\{R_\varepsilon(y)-1\}\,\omega(\dd y)
=\frac{4\varepsilon^2}{(4+\varepsilon^2)^2}.
\]
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Sharp analytic variance threshold for the leading KL coefficient]%
\begin{theorem}[Sharp analytic variance threshold for the leading KL coefficient]%
\label{thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold}
Let \(\nu\) have finite mean \(\bar{\gamma}\), and put
\[
H(t)=D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma})).
\]
With the extended nonnegative convention
\eqref{eq:extended-kl-definition} for \(D_{\rm KL}\),
\[
H(t)\in[0,\infty]\qquad(t>0).
\]
Then
\[
\EE X_c^2<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)<\infty .
\]
When the variance is finite,
\[
H(t)<\infty\qquad(t>0)
\]
and
\[
\lim_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)=\frac{(\EE X_c^2)^2}{8},
\]
and when the variance is infinite,
\[
\lim_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)=+\infty .
\]
The last limit is meant in the extended sense; no finite-time finiteness of
\(H(t)\) is asserted in the infinite-variance case, and the conclusion
includes the case \(H(t)=+\infty\) along an unbounded set of times.
No compact-support, exponential-tail, or regular-variation hypothesis is
assumed; finite variance is exactly one of the two equivalent conclusions.
This is the unconditional analytic iff asserted in this paper.
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{remark}
\begin{remark}
This theorem supplies the threshold clause in Theorem~\ref{thm:main-A}.
Its proof uses the coefficient theorem only in the finite-variance direction;
the infinite-variance direction is a bounded-test lower bound and does not
assert a finite Laurent expansion.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Multidimensional quotient and covariance package}
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Local multidimensional quotient--Taylor remainder]%
\begin{lemma}[Local multidimensional quotient--Taylor remainder]%
\label{lem:local-multidim-quotient-taylor-remainder-core}
Let \(d\ge1\), and let
\[
F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^{(d+1)/2}.
\]
For a multiindex \(\alpha\), put
\[
U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)=\frac1{\alpha!}\partial_z^\alpha F_y(0).
\]
For each integer \(L\ge2\) there is a constant \(C_{d,L}<\infty\),
depending only on \(d\) and \(L\), such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-multidim-coefficient-bound}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\le C_{d,L}
\qquad (|\alpha|\le L),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-multidim-taylor-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L+1}
\qquad (|z|\le1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Parity of multidimensional Cayley--Poisson modes]%
\begin{lemma}[Parity of multidimensional Cayley--Poisson modes]%
\label{lem:multidim-cayley-poisson-mode-parity}
For every \(d\ge1\) and every multiindex \(\alpha\in\NN_0^d\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-mode-parity}
U_\alpha^{(d)}(-y)=(-1)^{|\alpha|}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)
\qquad(y\in\RR^d).
\end{equation}
Consequently, for any multiindices
\(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-product-parity}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)=0
\qquad\text{if }\ |\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|\text{ is odd}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Global multidimensional quotient remainder]%
\begin{lemma}[Global multidimensional quotient remainder]%
\label{lem:global-multidim-quotient-remainder-core}
Let \(d\ge1\), \(a=(d+1)/2\), and
\[
F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^a .
\]
For a multiindex \(\alpha\), put
\[
U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)=\frac1{\alpha!}\partial_z^\alpha F_y(0).
\]
If \(L\in\mathbb N\) and \(L\ge d+1\), then there is a constant
\(C_{d,L}<\infty\), depending only on \(d\) and \(L\), such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-coefficient-bound}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\le C_{d,L}
\qquad (|\alpha|\le L),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-small-translation-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L+1}
\qquad (|z|\le1),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-large-translation-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L}
\qquad (|z|>1).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, for all \(y,z\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-global-translation-remainder}
\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}\left(
|z|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|z|\le1\}}
{}
+{}|z|^L{\bf1}_{\{|z|>1\}}
\right).
\end{equation}
Consequently, if \(X\) is an \(\RR^d\)-valued random vector with
\(\EE|X|^L<\infty\), \(\Omega_d\) is the Cauchy probability measure above,
and
\[
r_t(y):=\EE\left[
F_y(X/t)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)(X/t)^\alpha
\right],
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-integrated-remainder-bound}
\|r_t\|_{L^\infty(\RR^d)}+\|r_t\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}
\le C_{d,L}t^{-L}\EE\left[
|X|^L\frac{|X|}{t}{\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}
{}
+{}|X|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X|>t\}}
\right]=o(t^{-L}).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{definition}[Large-\(t\) entropy-existence clause]%
\begin{definition}[Large-\(t\) entropy-existence clause]%
\label{def:multidim-ex-dl}
For the above \(h_t,g_t,\delta_t^{(d)}\), the condition
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) means that there is \(T<\infty\) such that, for every
\(t\ge T\),
\[
1+\delta_t^{(d)}(y)>0,\qquad
\int_{\RR^d}\delta_t^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)=0,\qquad
\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)}(y))\,\Omega_d(\dd y)<\infty,
\]
and that the matched KL integral is represented by
\[
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
=\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)}(y))\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
The symbol records only these large-\(t\) positivity, zero-mass,
integrability, and change-of-variables assertions; it is not a moment
hypothesis and it is not implied by the finite-covariance algebra below.
\end{definition}

\subsubsection*{Multidimensional proof route}\leavevmode\par\smallskip
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{definition}[Two-region remainder functional]%
\begin{definition}[Two-region remainder functional]%
\label{def:two-region-remainder-functional}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\).  Put
\(X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\).  For \(t>0\), define \(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)\) by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:conditional-multidim-two-region-tail}
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)
&:=t^{-(L+1)}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|\le t\}}\right] \\
&\quad{}+ t^{-L}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|>t\}}\right],
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Two-region remainder is finite \(L\)-moment]%
\begin{lemma}[Two-region remainder is finite \(L\)-moment]%
\label{lem:two-region-remainder-equivalent-finite-moment}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\).  Put
\(X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\).  Then
\[
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})
\qquad(t\to\infty)
\]
is equivalent to \(\EE|X_c|^L<\infty\), with the convention that
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)<\infty\) for all sufficiently large \(t\).
This is the only two-region equivalence used in the multidimensional
finite-moment theorem.
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Explicit two-region quotient remainder estimate]%
\begin{proposition}[Explicit two-region quotient remainder estimate]%
\label{thm:multidimensional-conditional-tail-density-quotient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\).  Put
\(X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\).  Assume
\[
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L}).
\]
Equivalently, by Lemma~\ref{lem:two-region-remainder-equivalent-finite-moment},
this is exactly the finite \(L\)-moment condition
\(\EE|X_c|^L<\infty\).  Define
\[
\begin{gathered}
\mu_\alpha=\int_{\RR^d}X_c^\alpha\,\dd\nu(\gamma)
\qquad(|\alpha|\le L).
\end{gathered}
\]
Then, for
\[
\delta_t^{(d)}(y):=
\frac{(P_t^{(d)}*\nu)(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}
{P_t^{(d)}(ty)}-1,
\]
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-density-quotient-expansion-main}
\delta_t^{(d)}(y)=
\sum_{2\le|\alpha|\le L}\mu_\alpha U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)t^{-|\alpha|}
+\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}(y),
\end{equation}
where the remainder satisfies the explicit \(L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_d)\)
bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-density-quotient-explicit-remainder}
\begin{aligned}
\|\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\|_\infty+
\|\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}
&\le C_{d,L}\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)  \\
&=o(t^{-L}).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Moreover \(\|\delta_t^{(d)}\|_\infty=O(t^{-2})\), so
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) holds for all sufficiently large \(t\).
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Finite-moment supply of the quotient jet]%
\begin{theorem}[Finite-moment supply of the quotient jet]%
\label{thm:multidimensional-density-quotient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite
centred \(L\)th absolute moment.  Then
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})\), and the conclusions of
Proposition~\ref{thm:multidimensional-conditional-tail-density-quotient}
hold.
In particular \(\nu\) satisfies \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\) and
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) for all sufficiently large \(t\).
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Multidimensional quotient-to-KL transfer]%
\begin{proposition}[Multidimensional quotient-to-KL transfer]%
\label{thm:multidimensional-poisson-entropy-jets}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge2\), let \(\nu\) be a
probability law on \(\RR^d\), and assume that \((\nu,d,L)\) satisfies both
\((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\) and \((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\).  Thus
\[
\delta_t^{(d)}(y)
=\sum_{2\le\lvert\alpha\rvert\le L}
\mu_\alpha U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)t^{-\lvert\alpha\rvert}
+\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}(y),
\]
with
\[
\lVert\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\rVert_\infty+
\lVert\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\rVert_{L^1(\Omega_d)}=o(t^{-L}),
\qquad
\lVert\delta_t^{(d)}\rVert_\infty=O(t^{-2}).
\]
Then
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\bigl(P_t^{(d)}*\nu\,\|\,P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
=\sum_{m=4}^{L+2}\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)t^{-m}
+o(t^{-(L+2)}),
\]
where
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\in\NN_0^d\\
|\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|=m\\
2\le|\alpha_j|\le L\ \mathrm{for}\ 1\le j\le r}}
\mu_{\alpha_1}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_r}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
Moreover, all odd retained coefficients vanish:
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=0
\qquad\text{for every odd }m,\quad 4\le m\le L+2.
\]
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{remark}
\begin{remark}
For \(d=1\), \(\Sigma_0=0\), \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma=\mu_2\), and the
quadratic form evaluated below gives \(c_1^{\rm iso}=1/8\).  Therefore, once
the quotient-jet hypothesis of this proposition is in force,
\(\mathcal A_4^{(1)}=A_4=\mu_2^2/8\), matching the one-dimensional
Cayley--Chebyshev coefficient.
\end{remark}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{proposition}[Explicit-remainder form of the multidimensional KL jet]%
\begin{proposition}[Explicit-remainder form of the multidimensional KL jet]%
\label{thm:multidim-conditional-tail-poisson-kl-asymptotic}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\).  Put
\(X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\).  Assume
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})\); equivalently,
\(\EE|X_c|^L<\infty\).  Set
\[
\begin{aligned}
h_t&=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\\
g_t&=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),
\end{aligned}
\]
and define
\[
\mu_\alpha=\EE[X_c^\alpha]\qquad(|\alpha|\le L),
\]
which are finite by
Lemma~\ref{lem:two-region-remainder-equivalent-finite-moment}.  Then
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=\sum_{m=4}^{L+2}\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)t^{-m}
+o(t^{-(L+2)}),
\]
where
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\in\NN_0^d\\
|\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|=m\\
2\le|\alpha_j|\le L\ \mathrm{for}\ 1\le j\le r}}
\mu_{\alpha_1}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_r}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
All odd retained coefficients vanish:
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=0
\qquad\text{for every odd }m,\quad 4\le m\le L+2.
\]
\end{proposition}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{corollary}[Finite-moment multidimensional KL jet]%
\begin{corollary}[Finite-moment multidimensional KL jet]%
\label{thm:multidim-finite-moment-poisson-kl-asymptotic}%
\label{cor:multidim-finite-moment-entropy-jet}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred
\(L\)th absolute moment.  Set
\[
\begin{aligned}
h_t&=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\\
g_t&=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),
\end{aligned}
\]
and define the centred variable and moments by
\[
\begin{aligned}
X_c&=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},\\
\mu_\alpha&=\EE[X_c^\alpha]\qquad(|\alpha|\le L).
\end{aligned}
\]
Then
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=\sum_{m=4}^{L+2}\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)t^{-m}
+o(t^{-(L+2)}),
\]
where
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\in\NN_0^d\\
|\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|=m\\
2\le|\alpha_j|\le L\ \mathrm{for}\ 1\le j\le r}}
\mu_{\alpha_1}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_r}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
All odd retained coefficients vanish:
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=0
\qquad\text{for every odd }m,\quad 4\le m\le L+2.
\]
\end{corollary}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{remark}[Coefficient routes in Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}]
\begin{remark}[Coefficient routes in Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}]
Corollary~\ref{thm:multidim-finite-moment-poisson-kl-asymptotic} proves
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}.  Proposition
\ref{thm:multidim-conditional-tail-poisson-kl-asymptotic} is the same
finite-moment assertion written with the explicit remainder
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}\), whose \(o(t^{-L})\) decay is equivalent to finite
\(L\)-moment by
Lemma~\ref{lem:two-region-remainder-equivalent-finite-moment}.  The leading
coefficient route is the one stated at the start of this subsection.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{Covariance algebra}\leavevmode\par\smallskip
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[Radial--angular contractions for the Cauchy weight]%
\begin{lemma}[Radial--angular contractions for the Cauchy weight]%
\label{lem:radial-angular-contractions}
Let \(Y\) have law
\(\Omega_d(\dd y)=P_1^{(d)}(y)\dd y\), write the polar decomposition
\[
Y=\sqrt R\,\Theta,\qquad R=|Y|^2,\qquad \Theta\in S^{d-1}.
\]
Then \(R\) and \(\Theta\) are independent, \(\Theta\) is uniform on
\(S^{d-1}\), and \(R\) has beta-prime law with parameters \(d/2\) and
\(1/2\).  The angular fourth moments are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:spherical-fourth-contraction}
\EE_\Theta[\Theta_i\Theta_j\Theta_k\Theta_l]
=
\frac{\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}
+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}
+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}}{d(d+2)}
\qquad(1\le i,j,k,l\le d).
\end{equation}
Consequently, for real symmetric matrices \(A,B\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:spherical-quadratic-contraction}
\EE_\Theta[
(\Theta^{\mathsf T}A\Theta)(\Theta^{\mathsf T}B\Theta)]
=
\frac{(\operatorname{tr}A)(\operatorname{tr}B)
+2\operatorname{tr}(AB)}{d(d+2)}.
\end{equation}
In particular, if \(A\) is traceless, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:spherical-traceless-square}
\EE_\Theta[(\Theta^{\mathsf T}A\Theta)^2]
=\frac{2}{d(d+2)}\|A\|_{\rm HS}^2 .
\end{equation}
For every real \(k\) with \(-d/2<k<9/2\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:radial-beta-prime-contraction-general}
\EE\frac{R^k}{(1+R)^4}
=
\frac{B(d/2+k,\,9/2-k)}{B(d/2,\,1/2)}
=
\frac{\Gamma(d/2+k)\Gamma(9/2-k)}
{\Gamma(d/2)\Gamma(1/2)}
\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}{\Gamma((d+9)/2)} .
\end{equation}
Thus, with \(D_d=(d+1)(d+3)(d+5)(d+7)\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:radial-beta-prime-contractions-low}
\EE\frac{1}{(1+R)^4}=\frac{105}{D_d},\qquad
\EE\frac{R}{(1+R)^4}=\frac{15d}{D_d},\qquad
\EE\frac{R^2}{(1+R)^4}=\frac{3d(d+2)}{D_d}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\subsubsection*{Algebraic covariance layer}\leavevmode\par\smallskip
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Covariance algebra under finite covariance]%
\begin{theorem}[Covariance algebra under finite covariance]%
\label{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra}
\label{prop:multidim-formal-covariance-layer}
Let \(d\ge1\), and let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with
finite centred second moment.  Put
\[
\bar{\gamma}=\int_{\RR^d}\gamma\,\dd\nu(\gamma),
\qquad
X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},
\]
and
\[
\Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad
\Sigma_0=\Sigma-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{d}I_d.
\]
The degree-two centred density mode associated with \(\Sigma\) is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-leading-theorem-density-mode}
b_\Sigma(y)=
\frac{d+1}{2}\left(
-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{1+|y|^2}
+\frac{(d+3)y^{\mathsf T}\Sigma y}{(1+|y|^2)^2}
\right).
\end{equation}
Define its algebraic quadratic entropy-integrand layer by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-leading-positive-defect}
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
:=\frac12\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)^2\,\Omega_d(\dd y)
=c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2,
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-leading-positive-constants}
c_d^{\rm iso}=
\frac{3(d+1)(7d+9)}
{4d(d+3)(d+5)(d+7)},
\qquad
c_d^{\rm tr}=
\frac{3(d+1)(d+3)}
{4(d+5)(d+7)} .
\end{equation}
Two immediate checks are
\[
d=1:\qquad \Sigma_0=0,\qquad c_1^{\rm iso}=\frac18,\qquad
\mathcal Q_1(\Sigma)=\frac{(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2}{8},
\]
and, for an isotropic covariance \(\Sigma=\tau I_d\),
\[
\Sigma_0=0,\qquad
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)=c_d^{\rm iso}d^2\tau^2 .
\]
This is the algebraic covariance calculation.  No KL asymptotic is asserted
in this theorem.  Lemma~\ref{lem:radial-angular-contractions} gives the radial
and angular contractions entering the constants.
\end{theorem}

\subsubsection*{Second-order quotient bridge}\leavevmode\par\smallskip
### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{lemma}[\(L^2\) second-order entropy criterion]%
\begin{lemma}[\(L^2\) second-order entropy criterion]%
\label{lem:l2-second-order-entropy-criterion}
Let \((X,\mathcal A,\lambda)\) be a probability space and let
\(\delta_t:X\to[-1,\infty)\) be measurable with
\(\int_X\delta_t\,\dd\lambda=0\).  Let \(b\in L^2(\lambda)\), and put
\[
  \Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s .
\]
Assume only
\[
  t^2\delta_t\longrightarrow b
  \qquad\text{in }L^2(\lambda).
\]
Then
\[
  \int_X\Phi(\delta_t)\,\dd\lambda
  =\frac{t^{-4}}2\int_X b^2\,\dd\lambda+o(t^{-4}).
\]
\end{lemma}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{theorem}[Second-order quotient criterion for the KL coefficient]%
\begin{theorem}[Second-order quotient criterion for the KL coefficient]%
\label{thm:second-order-quotient-kl-coefficient}
\label{prop:second-order-quotient-criterion}
Let \(d\ge1\), and let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with
mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred covariance \(\Sigma\).  Put
\[
  h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
  g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\qquad
  \delta_t^{(d)}(y)=\frac{h_t(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}
  {g_t(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}-1 .
\]
Assume the separate second-order quotient criterion
\begin{equation}\label{eq:second-order-quotient-criterion}
  \delta_t^{(d)}(y)=t^{-2}b_\Sigma(y)+r_t(y),\qquad
  \|r_t\|_\infty+\|r_t\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}=o(t^{-2}),\qquad
  \|\delta_t^{(d)}\|_\infty=O(t^{-2})
\end{equation}
with \(b_\Sigma\) as in
\eqref{eq:multidim-leading-theorem-density-mode}, and assume that the matched
KL integral is represented by
\[
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
=\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)}(y))\,\Omega_d(\dd y)
\]
for all sufficiently large \(t\).  Then
\[
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}).
\]
The same conclusion also follows from the non-uniform criterion
\[
  t^2\delta_t^{(d)}\to b_\Sigma\quad\text{in }L^2(\Omega_d)
\]
provided the KL integral is represented by
\(\int\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)})\,\dd\Omega_d\) for all sufficiently large \(t\).
\end{theorem}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{corollary}[Actual leading multidimensional KL coefficient]%
\begin{corollary}[Actual leading multidimensional KL coefficient]%
\label{cor:finite-dplusone-moment-leading-multidim-coefficient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean
\(\bar{\gamma}\), and assume
\[
  \EE |X_c|^{d+1}<\infty,\qquad X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma} .
\]
Put
\[
  h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
  g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\qquad
  \Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad
  \Sigma_0=\Sigma-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{d}I_d .
\]
Then Theorem~\ref{thm:multidimensional-density-quotient} supplies the
second-order quotient criterion
\eqref{eq:second-order-quotient-criterion}.  Hence
\[
D_{\rm KL}\!\left(P_t^{(d)}*\nu\,\middle\|\,
P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma})\right)
=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}),
\]
where
\[
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
=c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2
\]
and
\[
c_d^{\rm iso}=
\frac{3(d+1)(7d+9)}
{4d(d+3)(d+5)(d+7)},
\qquad
c_d^{\rm tr}=
\frac{3(d+1)(d+3)}
{4(d+5)(d+7)} .
\]
\end{corollary}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{corollary}[Internal bridge: leading coefficient under \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})+(\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\)]%
\begin{corollary}[Internal bridge: leading coefficient under \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})+(\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\)]%
\label{cor:multidim-leading-kl-coefficient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge2\), and assume that
\((\nu,d,L)\) satisfies both \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\) and
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\).  Let
\[
h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}).
\]
Since \(L\ge2\), the covariance
\[
\Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},
\]
exists.  Then
\[
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}).
\]
\end{corollary}

### sec_entropy_core_main.tex: \begin{corollary}[Finite-moment covariance coefficient for larger \(L\)]%
\begin{corollary}[Finite-moment covariance coefficient for larger \(L\)]%
\label{cor:multidim-finite-moment-covariance-coefficient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite
centred \(L\)th absolute moment.  Put
\[
h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),
\]
and let
\[
X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},\qquad
\Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad
\Sigma_0=\Sigma-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{d}I_d .
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-moment-covariance-coefficient-through-mpj}
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}),
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\) is the quadratic covariance contraction computed
in Theorem~\ref{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra}:
\[
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
=c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2 .
\]
\end{corollary}

\[
B(t):=
\int_{\RR}u_2(y)\delta_t(y)\,\omega(\dd y)
=

and the right side tends to

\(M\to\infty\) proves divergence when the variance is infinite.

\subsection{Multidimensional quotient and covariance package}

For \(d\ge1\), let
\[
P_t^{(d)}(x)=c_d\,\frac{t}{(t^2+|x|^2)^{(d+1)/2}},
\qquad
c_d=\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}{\pi^{(d+1)/2}},
\qquad
\Omega_d(\dd y)=P_1^{(d)}(y)\dd y.
\]
Thus \(P_t^{(d)}\) is normalized to have total mass one and
\[
P_t^{(d)}(ty)t^d\,\dd y=\Omega_d(\dd y)
=c_d(1+|y|^2)^{-(d+1)/2}\dd y .
\]
For \(z\in\RR^d\), write
\[
F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^{(d+1)/2}.
\]
Let \(U_\alpha^{(d)}\) be the Taylor coefficients of \(F_y(z)\) at \(z=0\).

\begin{lemma}[Local multidimensional quotient--Taylor remainder]%
\label{lem:local-multidim-quotient-taylor-remainder-core}
Let \(d\ge1\), and let
\[
F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^{(d+1)/2}.
\]
For a multiindex \(\alpha\), put
\[
U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)=\frac1{\alpha!}\partial_z^\alpha F_y(0).
\]
For each integer \(L\ge2\) there is a constant \(C_{d,L}<\infty\),
depending only on \(d\) and \(L\), such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-multidim-coefficient-bound}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\le C_{d,L}
\qquad (|\alpha|\le L),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-multidim-taylor-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L+1}
\qquad (|z|\le1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\ifdefined\JFAArticleMain
\relocatedproof{sec_entropy_core_main.tex}{the proof following Lemma
``Local multidimensional quotient--Taylor remainder''}
\else
\begin{proof}
Put \(a=(d+1)/2\).  For each multiindex \(\beta\), repeated
differentiation in \(z\) gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-multidim-derivative-structure}
\partial_z^\beta F_y(w)
=(1+|y|^2)^a
\frac{P_\beta(y-w)}{(1+|y-w|^2)^{a+|\beta|}},
\end{equation}
where \(P_\beta\) is a polynomial of degree at most \(|\beta|\), depending
only on \(d\) and \(\beta\).  Indeed, \(z\)-differentiation is
\(-\xi\)-differentiation for \(\xi=y-z\), and each differentiation of
\((1+|\xi|^2)^{-b}\) raises the denominator exponent by one and multiplies
by a polynomial whose degree increases by at most one.

If \(|w|\le1\) and \(|y|>2\), then \(|y-w|\ge |y|/2\), and
\[
|\partial_z^\beta F_y(w)|
\le C_{d,\beta}
(1+|y|^2)^a
\frac{(1+|y|)^{|\beta|}}{(1+|y|^2)^{a+|\beta|}}
\le C_{d,\beta}.
\]
If \(|y|\le2\), the same derivative is bounded uniformly for \(|w|\le1\)
because the denominator in \eqref{eq:local-multidim-derivative-structure}
is at least \(1\).  Thus
\[
\sup_{\substack{y\in\RR^d,\ |w|\le1\\ |\beta|\le L+1}}
|\partial_z^\beta F_y(w)|<\infty .
\]
Taking \(w=0\) and \(|\beta|\le L\) proves
\eqref{eq:local-multidim-coefficient-bound}.  The multivariable Taylor
formula at \(z=0\), with integral remainder and the displayed derivative
bound for \(|\beta|=L+1\), gives
\eqref{eq:local-multidim-taylor-remainder}.
\end{proof}
\fi

\ifdefined\JFAArticleMain
The parity identities, global two-region quotient estimate, matched quotient
jet definitions, finite-moment supply theorem, and tensor coefficient transfer
are proved in \path{sec_entropy_core_main.tex}, subsection
``Multidimensional quotient and covariance package,'' as compiled by
\path{supplement_relocated_support.tex}.  The local Taylor bound above and
the covariance and second-order entropy bridges below are the parts used by
the sharp dimensional results retained in the article.
\else
\begin{lemma}[Parity of multidimensional Cayley--Poisson modes]%
\label{lem:multidim-cayley-poisson-mode-parity}
For every \(d\ge1\) and every multiindex \(\alpha\in\NN_0^d\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-mode-parity}
U_\alpha^{(d)}(-y)=(-1)^{|\alpha|}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)
\qquad(y\in\RR^d).
\end{equation}
Consequently, for any multiindices
\(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-product-parity}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)=0
\qquad\text{if }\ |\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|\text{ is odd}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The defining quotient satisfies
\[
F_{-y}(z)
=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|-y-z|^2}\right)^{(d+1)/2}
=F_y(-z).
\]
Both sides are real analytic in \(z\) near \(0\).  Comparing the Taylor
coefficients in
\[
F_{-y}(z)=\sum_{\alpha}U_\alpha^{(d)}(-y)z^\alpha,
\qquad
F_y(-z)=\sum_{\alpha}(-1)^{|\alpha|}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\]
gives \eqref{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-mode-parity}.  Multiplying this
identity for \(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\) gives
\[
\prod_{j=1}^r U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(-y)
=(-1)^{|\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|}
\prod_{j=1}^r U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y).
\]
Since \(\Omega_d(\dd y)=c_d(1+|y|^2)^{-(d+1)/2}\dd y\) is invariant under
\(y\mapsto-y\), the integral of the product is equal to its negative when
the total order is odd, proving
\eqref{eq:multidim-cayley-poisson-product-parity}.
\end{proof}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Deterministic quotient remainder.}
The next lemma is the analytic estimate used later to verify the named
matched quotient jet from finite moments.  The quotient jet itself, including
the entropy-existence clause, is defined in full immediately before the
transfer proposition so the analytic input is visible at the point where it is
used.

\begin{lemma}[Global multidimensional quotient remainder]%
\label{lem:global-multidim-quotient-remainder-core}
Let \(d\ge1\), \(a=(d+1)/2\), and
\[
F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^a .
\]
For a multiindex \(\alpha\), put
\[
U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)=\frac1{\alpha!}\partial_z^\alpha F_y(0).
\]
If \(L\in\mathbb N\) and \(L\ge d+1\), then there is a constant
\(C_{d,L}<\infty\), depending only on \(d\) and \(L\), such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-coefficient-bound}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\le C_{d,L}
\qquad (|\alpha|\le L),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-small-translation-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L+1}
\qquad (|z|\le1),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-large-translation-remainder}
\sup_{y\in\RR^d}\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}|z|^{L}
\qquad (|z|>1).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, for all \(y,z\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-global-translation-remainder}
\left|
F_y(z)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha
\right|
\le C_{d,L}\left(
|z|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|z|\le1\}}
{}
+{}|z|^L{\bf1}_{\{|z|>1\}}
\right).
\end{equation}
Consequently, if \(X\) is an \(\RR^d\)-valued random vector with
\(\EE|X|^L<\infty\), \(\Omega_d\) is the Cauchy probability measure above,
and
\[
r_t(y):=\EE\left[
F_y(X/t)-\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)(X/t)^\alpha
\right],
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:multidim-integrated-remainder-bound}
\|r_t\|_{L^\infty(\RR^d)}+\|r_t\|_{L^1(\Omega_d)}
\le C_{d,L}t^{-L}\EE\left[
|X|^L\frac{|X|}{t}{\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}
{}
+{}|X|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X|>t\}}
\right]=o(t^{-L}).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The coefficient bound \eqref{eq:multidim-coefficient-bound} and the
small-translation remainder
\eqref{eq:multidim-small-translation-remainder} are exactly
Lemma~\ref{lem:local-multidim-quotient-taylor-remainder-core}.  Thus the
constant in these two estimates depends only on \(d,L\).

It remains to handle large translations.  Fix \(|z|>1\).  First suppose
\(|y-z|\le |z|/2\).  Then \(|y|\le3|z|/2\), and hence
\[
F_y(z)\le (1+|y|^2)^a\le C_d|z|^{d+1}.
\]
On the complementary region \(|y-z|>|z|/2\), the elementary estimate
\[
1+|y|^2
\le 2(1+|y-z|^2+|z|^2)
\le 2(1+|z|^2)(1+|y-z|^2)
\]
gives
\[
F_y(z)
\le 2^a(1+|z|^2)^a
\le C_d|z|^{d+1}
\qquad(|z|>1).
\]
Thus, uniformly in \(y\),
\[
\sup_y F_y(z)\le C_d|z|^{d+1}\le C_{d,L}|z|^L
\qquad(|z|>1),
\]
where the last inequality is precisely where \(L\ge d+1\) is used.  The
coefficient bound from the local lemma gives, again uniformly in \(y\),
\[
\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\,|z|^{|\alpha|}
\le C_{d,L}\sum_{k=0}^{L}|z|^k
\le C_{d,L}'|z|^L
\qquad(|z|>1).
\]
Consequently
\[
\sup_y\left(
|F_y(z)|
+\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}|U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)|\,|z|^{|\alpha|}
\right)
\le C_{d,L}|z|^L
\qquad(|z|>1),
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:multidim-large-translation-remainder}.  Combining
this estimate with the small-translation remainder proves the global form
\eqref{eq:multidim-global-translation-remainder}.

Set \(z=X/t\) in \eqref{eq:multidim-global-translation-remainder}.  Taking
expectations gives the pointwise bound, uniform in \(y\),
\[
|r_t(y)|
\le
C_{d,L}\left[
t^{-(L+1)}
\EE\!\left(|X|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}\right)
+t^{-L}
\EE\!\left(|X|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X|>t\}}\right)
\right].
\]
Equivalently,
\[
t^{-(L+1)}
\EE\!\left(|X|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}\right)
=
t^{-L}
\EE\!\left[
|X|^L\frac{|X|}{t}{\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}
\right]
=o(t^{-L}).
\]
because \((|X|/t){\bf1}_{\{|X|\le t\}}\le1\) and converges pointwise to
zero, while \(|X|^L\) is integrable.  Also
\[
t^{-L}\EE\!\left(|X|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X|>t\}}\right)=o(t^{-L})
\]
by the absolute continuity of the integral for the integrable function
\(|X|^L\).  These two estimates are independent of \(y\), and therefore give
the \(L^\infty\) part of \eqref{eq:multidim-integrated-remainder-bound}.
The \(L^1(\Omega_d)\) part follows from the same uniform bound because
\(\Omega_d\) is a probability measure.
\end{proof}

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Matched multidimensional quotient input.}
For \(d\ge1\) and \(L\in\mathbb N\) with \(L\ge2\), put
\[
h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\qquad
\delta_t^{(d)}(y)=
\frac{h_t(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}{g_t(\bar{\gamma}+ty)}-1,
\]
and \(\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s\).

\begin{definition}[Large-\(t\) entropy-existence clause]%
\label{def:multidim-ex-dl}
For the above \(h_t,g_t,\delta_t^{(d)}\), the condition
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) means that there is \(T<\infty\) such that, for every
\(t\ge T\),
\[
1+\delta_t^{(d)}(y)>0,\qquad
\int_{\RR^d}\delta_t^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y)=0,\qquad
\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)}(y))\,\Omega_d(\dd y)<\infty,
\]
and that the matched KL integral is represented by
\[
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
=\int_{\RR^d}\Phi(\delta_t^{(d)}(y))\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
The symbol records only these large-\(t\) positivity, zero-mass,
integrability, and change-of-variables assertions; it is not a moment
hypothesis and it is not implied by the finite-covariance algebra below.
\end{definition}

We say that \((\nu,d,L)\) satisfies the matched Poisson quotient jet
\((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\) when \(\nu\) is a probability law on \(\RR^d\), the
mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) exists, the centred tensor moments
\[
\mu_\alpha=\int_{\RR^d}(\gamma-\bar{\gamma})^\alpha\,\dd\nu(\gamma)
\qquad(2\le|\alpha|\le L)
\]
exist, and the scaled quotient has the \(L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_d)\) jet
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mpj-dl-definition}
\begin{aligned}
(\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L}):\qquad
\delta_t^{(d)}(y)
&=
\sum_{2\le\lvert\alpha\rvert\le L}
\mu_\alpha U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)t^{-\lvert\alpha\rvert}
+\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}(y),\\
\lVert\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\rVert_\infty+
\lVert\rho_{L,t}^{(d)}\rVert_{L^1(\Omega_d)}
&=o(t^{-L}),\qquad
\lVert\delta_t^{(d)}\rVert_\infty=O(t^{-2}).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Thus the input used in
Proposition~\ref{thm:multidimensional-poisson-entropy-jets} has two parts:
the quotient jet \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\) and the entropy-existence clause
\((\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\).  The restriction \(L\ge d+1\) enters when the
large-translation estimates are closed by the finite \(L\)-moment remainder
estimate below.

\subsubsection*{Multidimensional proof route}\leavevmode\par\smallskip
The multidimensional proof has one analytic bridge.  For \(L\ge d+1\),
\[
\EE|X_c|^L<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
(\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})+(\mathrm{EX}_{d,L}),
\]
and then Proposition~\ref{thm:multidimensional-poisson-entropy-jets} gives the
KL tensor jet.  At order four this jet has
\[
\mathcal A_4^{(d)}(\nu)
=\frac12\int_{\RR^d}(D_2^{(d)})^2\,\dd\Omega_d
=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma),
\]
where the last equality is the covariance algebra proved below.  Thus finite
\(L\)-moment supplies the quotient/existence bridge and hence the actual
leading KL coefficient.  Finite covariance by itself supplies only the
algebraic quantity \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\).

The equivalence displayed in this proof route refers only to the functional
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}\) defined next.  It is not a statement that every
two-region estimate, or every possible quotient remainder norm, is equivalent
to finite \(L\)-moment.

\begin{definition}[Two-region remainder functional]%
\label{def:two-region-remainder-functional}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\).  Put
\(X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}\).  For \(t>0\), define \(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)\) by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:conditional-multidim-two-region-tail}
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)
&:=t^{-(L+1)}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|\le t\}}\right] \\
&\quad{}+ t^{-L}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|>t\}}\right],
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\[
\frac12\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)^2\,\Omega_d(\dd y)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma).
\]
Thus \(D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4})\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Finite-moment covariance coefficient for larger \(L\)]%
\label{cor:multidim-finite-moment-covariance-coefficient}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be
a probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite
centred \(L\)th absolute moment.  Put
\[
h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),
\]
and let
\[
X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma},\qquad
\Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad
\Sigma_0=\Sigma-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{d}I_d .
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-moment-covariance-coefficient-through-mpj}
D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}),
\end{equation}
where \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\) is the quadratic covariance contraction computed
in Theorem~\ref{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra}:
\[
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
=c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2 .
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:multidimensional-density-quotient} turns the finite centred
\(L\)-absolute moment assumption into \((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})\), and
Proposition~\ref{thm:multidimensional-poisson-entropy-jets} then gives the KL
coefficient jet.  The \(m=4\) coefficient in that jet is
\[
\mathcal A_4^{(d)}(\nu)
=\frac12\int_{\RR^d}D_2^{(d)}(y;\nu)^2\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
The degree-two density layer depends only on the covariance:
\[
D_2^{(d)}(y;\nu)=
\frac{d+1}{2}\left(
-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{1+|y|^2}
+\frac{(d+3)y^{\mathsf T}\Sigma y}{(1+|y|^2)^2}
\right).
\]
Theorem~\ref{thm:multidim-covariance-algebra} evaluates the last
quadratic integral as \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\), proving the asserted leading
term.
\end{proof}

\noindent\emph{Sharpness of the uniform quotient clause.}
The threshold \(L\ge d+1\) is sharp for convergence in the
\(L^\infty\) quotient topology.  Indeed, fix an integer
\(1\le L<d+1\), let \(e_1\in\RR^d\), and choose \(R_n\uparrow\infty\) so
fast that
\[
  W:=\sum_{n\ge1}R_n^{-L}n^{-2}<1,
  \qquad
  t_n:=8^{-1/(d+1)}
  R_n^{(d+1-L)/(d+1)}n^{-2/(d+1)}\to\infty,
\]
and set \(w_n=R_n^{-L}n^{-2}\).  The symmetric probability law
\[
  \nu=(1-W)\delta_0
  +\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{w_n}{2}
  \bigl(\delta_{R_ne_1}+\delta_{-R_ne_1}\bigr)
\]
has mean zero and finite \(L\)th absolute moment because
\(\sum_n w_nR_n^L=\sum_n n^{-2}<\infty\).  At the space-time point
\(x_n=R_ne_1\), with \(g_t=P_t^{(d)}\) and \(h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu\),
\[
\frac{h_{t_n}(x_n)}{g_{t_n}(x_n)}
\ge
\frac{w_n}{2}\,
\frac{P_{t_n}^{(d)}(0)}{P_{t_n}^{(d)}(R_ne_1)}
\ge
\frac{w_n}{2}\left(\frac{R_n}{t_n}\right)^{d+1}
=4 .
\]
Hence \(\|\delta_{t_n}^{(d)}\|_\infty\ge3\) along \(t_n\to\infty\), so
the quotient does not even converge uniformly to one.  Thus \(d+1\) is the
sharp moment threshold for the uniform quotient route used above.  This
counterexample does not show that \(d+1\) moments are necessary for the
leading KL coefficient under a weaker \(L^2(\Omega_d)\) or entropy topology.
Those topologies are treated separately in
Section~\ref{sec:verified-A2-results}: Theorem
\ref{thm:multidim-l2-moment-threshold} gives the sharp \(L^2\) exponent,
while Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold} gives the
optimal uniform sufficient KL exponent in the finite absolute-moment scale,
not a law-by-law moment criterion.

===== sec_preliminaries.tex =====
\section{Cayley--Haar preliminaries}%
\begin{lemma}[KL bounded-test and Poisson quotient estimates]%
\label{lem:kl-bounded-test-and-poisson-quotient}
\label{lem:bounded-test-pinsker}
\label{lem:finite-time-poisson-kl-finite}
\label{lem:sharp-threshold-bounded-test-pinsker}
The following elementary estimates will be used repeatedly.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Let \(f\) and \(g\) be probability densities on~\(\RR\), and interpret
\(D_{\mathrm{KL}}(f\|g)\) by \eqref{eq:extended-kl-definition}.  If
\(\varphi\in L^\infty(\RR)\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prelim-bounded-test-pinsker}
\left|\int_{\RR}\varphi(x)\{f(x)-g(x)\}\dd x\right|^2
\le 2\|\varphi\|_\infty^2D_{\mathrm{KL}}(f\|g).
\end{equation}
The inequality is an extended-valued statement: if the right-hand side is
\(+\infty\), it is read as a trivial bound.

\item Let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on~\(\RR\) with mean
\(\bar{\gamma}\) and finite centred second moment.  Define
\[
P_t(x):=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{t}{t^2+x^2},\qquad
h_t=P_t*\nu,\qquad g_t=P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\qquad u_t=h_t/g_t .
\]
For every compact interval \(0<a<b<\infty\), the quotient \(u_t\) is bounded
above and below by positive constants uniformly for
\((t,x)\in[a,b]\times\RR\).  More precisely, with
\(\mu_2=\int(\gamma-\bar\gamma)^2\dd\nu(\gamma)\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prelim-poisson-quotient-two-sided-bound}
\frac{1}{2(1+\mu_2/a^2)}
\le u_t(x)\le 2+\frac{2\mu_2}{a^2}
\qquad(t\in[a,b],\ x\in\RR).
\end{equation}

\item Under the hypotheses of the preceding item,
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)<\infty\qquad(t>0).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

===== sec_cayley_gate.tex =====
\subsection{The Cayley chart}\label{sec:cayley-gate}
\begin{proposition}[Cayley compactification]\label{prop:cayley-homeo}
The map $\iota$ extends to a homeomorphism
\[
\iota:\overline\RR\xrightarrow{\sim}\TT
\]
by $\iota(\infty)=-1$.  Its inverse is given by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cayley-inverse}
\iota^{-1}(e^{\ii\theta})=\tan\frac{\theta}{2},
\qquad
\theta\in(-\pi,\pi),
\qquad
\iota^{-1}(-1)=\infty.
\end{equation}
Moreover, $\upsilon:\RR\to\UU$ and
$\iota:\RR\to\TT\setminus\{-1\}$ are $C^\infty$ diffeomorphisms.
\end{proposition}

===== sec_haar_pullback.tex =====
\subsection{Pullback of Haar measure}%
\begin{proposition}[Cauchy phase Jacobian]\label{prop:phase-derivative}
The phase coordinate \eqref{eq:phase-coord} satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phase-deriv}
\upsilon'(x)=\frac{1}{\pi(1+x^{2})},
\qquad
\vartheta'(x)=\frac{2}{1+x^{2}}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, $\upsilon:(\RR,\omega)\to(\UU,\dd u)$ is measure preserving.
In particular, for every non-negative Borel function $f$ on~$\UU$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-integral}
\int_{\RR}f(\upsilon(x))\,\omega(\dd x)=\int_{\UU}f(u)\dd u.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Endpoint-normalized chart]\label{rem:haar-pullback-normalization}
For the Cayley map itself the measure statement can be written in the two
equivalent forms
\begin{equation}\label{eq:haar-pullback}
\iota_{\#}\omega=m_{\TT},
\qquad
\iota^{*}m_{\TT}:=(\iota^{-1})_{\#}m_{\TT}=\omega.
\end{equation}
Here \(\iota_{\#}\) denotes pushforward from the line compactification to
the circle, while the displayed pullback notation is used only in this
homeomorphic chart sense.
This identity is the normalization used later for every Cauchy-weighted
mode integral and Laurent constant-term extraction.  It also fixes the
endpoint-normalized chart: if an orientation-preserving homeomorphism
\(\varphi:\overline\RR\to\TT\) has \(C^1\) restriction to \(\RR\),
\(\varphi(0)=1\), \(\varphi(\infty)=-1\), and
\(\varphi_{\#}\omega=m_{\TT}\), then writing
\(\varphi(x)=e^{\ii\alpha(x)}\) with \(\alpha(0)=0\) gives
\((2\pi)^{-1}\alpha'(x)\dd x=\omega(\dd x)\), hence
\(\alpha(x)=2\arctan x\) and \(\varphi=\iota\).  Reversing orientation only
adds the conjugate chart.
\end{remark}
